Primary Thesisupdated

The older diagnosis still applies in shape, but production now has a projection/index read plane. Reads are mostly fast; the new battlefront is warm Session execution shape, write amplification, route/reply loopback, blob uploads, DBM correlation, continuous profiling, and projection drift proof.

Program Progress

Current status

This HTML file is now the living dashboard for the Temper latency and observability acceleration goal. It must be updated as milestones move, evidence arrives, tasks split, ADRs land, PRs open, PRs merge, live runs pass, and deployment completes.

99.6%
Progress correctionThe program is now about 99.6%, not complete. PERF-026 is accepted as a measured latency win after PR merge, Docker/Main CI, Railway deployment, live production proof, and fixed-version Datadog before/after measurement. PERF-027 moved through Temper PR #258, TemperPaw PR #305, post-merge CI/Docker, Railway deployment, live proof, and fixed-version Datadog after measurement, but is recorded as a deployed partial/negative latency result. PERF-028 has now also moved through PR TemperPaw #306, merge, main CI, Docker, Railway deployment, live proof, independent SessionEntry read-back, and fixed-version Datadog evidence; it is accepted as a correctness/observability improvement but not as a speed win because the after provider_response_applier envelope stayed slower than the PERF-027 baseline. PERF-029 has now moved through Temper PR #259, TemperPaw rollout PR #307, PR/main CI, Docker, Railway deployment, exact version proof, live Session proof, and fixed-version Datadog after-readout. PERF-029 is accepted as a deployed observability/attribution slice, not a claimed speed win; its job was to identify the next actual speed target. INC-002 and INC-003 remain production-accepted correctness/stability fixes, not latency wins.
Evidence ruleNew hard gate: no remaining performance improvement counts as complete unless this report records the before Datadog trace/query/window, the after fixed-version trace/query/window, p50/p95/p99 or exact span timings where applicable, correctness proof for any projection/cache/materialization change, PR/merge/deploy/version evidence, and the Datadog acceptance link or query. Correctness-only incident fixes such as INC-002 and INC-003 are accepted by live parity/read-back/control-plane proof plus Datadog span/log evidence; they are not counted as speed wins without controlled before/after latency data.
PhaseThe measurement and observability foundation is live, and thirty-three shipped slices have moved through deployment proof: ADR-0088/File $value fast path through PERF-029 WASM dispatch envelope attribution, plus INC-001, INC-002, and INC-003. PERF-027 closed the actor-hydration hypothesis for data-only SessionEntry creates: the fast path is correct and deployed, but Datadog says the next real residual is storage/projection idle time and write verification/coalescing, not actor CPU. PERF-028 removed the normal immediate filtered read-back from acknowledged SessionEntry creates and proved the ack contract in production, but Datadog says that read-back was not the dominant remaining span cost. PERF-029 now proves the platform dispatch envelope shape: the hot post-provider residual is mostly authorized secret resolution, engine invocation/handle, host-chain construction, and callback dispatch, not guest artifact reads.
Incident blockerResolved for production. The Codex streaming begin_outbound rc -4 regression was caused by the PERF-015 Temper LocalTDataWasmHost wrapper merged in Temper PR #251 and rolled out through TemperPaw PR #287: it delegated regular HTTP host methods but did not preserve the http_stream_* host contract. The permanent fix merged in Temper PR #255 as 92c2a728, rolled through TemperPaw PR #297 as 59489a93, deployed to Railway as sha-59489a9, and passed a live Codex streaming proof.

Before: production observed an impossible projection shape, All DesignLanguages = 6 while Published DesignLanguages = 173. After on sha-3b8dcc23: All DesignLanguages = 240, canonical Status filters sum to 240 (Draft 41, UnderReview 4, Published 170, Archived 25), and every filtered ID is present in the unfiltered result. The live proof shows no hot-module blob.transport.get spans on the declared Session path.

Root-span correctionPERF-021 selection found an important measurement caveat: Session.workflow currently waits through an intentional 2s observability drain in crates/temper-server/src/workflow_tracing.rs before the root span closes. Fixed-version roots around 2.9s therefore should not be read as pure product latency. The next measured work splits into two pieces: make the drain explicit in Datadog so root spans are not misread, and separately reduce the real staged Session integration/OData work.

Next gateOpen PERF-030 as a main-based Temper worktree with an ADR before code. The measured target is dispatch.wasm.phase.authz_secret_resolution: after-batch all-module avg 78.7 ms, p50 76.3 ms, p95 87.8 ms; provider-response-specific avg 78.0 ms, p50 73.4 ms, p95 79.4 ms. Secondary targets are engine_invoke_and_handle (provider-response avg 73.6 ms, of which engine_invoke averages 54.2 ms), host_chain_build (provider-response avg 36.0 ms), and dispatch_callback (provider-response avg 18.9 ms). The first measured speed hypothesis is a correctness-preserving, tenant-scoped fast path or memoized decision for integrations with no secret requirement, no secret payload, and unchanged Cedar/spec/secret generation; the before/after acceptance must reuse the PERF-029 fixed-version trace/span queries.

Started INC-003 in fresh TemperPaw worktree /Users/seshendranalla/Development/temperpaw-worktrees/session-entry-sequence-order-20260518, branch codex/session-entry-sequence-order-20260518, based on origin/main at 2f7f718b0a62817896884bc29345ae421d37cf3d. ADR-022 is written before the code change.

Root cause: next_session_entry_id interpreted the trailing 0 in generated first user ID u-<session_id>-0 as a SessionEntry sequence instead of a user-turn index. The patch keeps the readable ID shape but maps u-ss-...-0 to logical sequence 1, so the first assistant child becomes a-2 with Sequence=2.

2026-05-16 13:43 EDT

Packaging review found and fixed a concurrency edge in the first PERF-002 implementation. A missing catalog row cannot be locked with FOR UPDATE, so concurrent first-writer projection transactions could have diffed against an empty field index and left stale rows. The patch now upserts entity_catalog first so the catalog primary key serializes both new and existing entity updates before the transaction locks and diffs entity_field_index.

Re-verified after the transaction-order fix: cargo fmt --all -- --check, cargo check -p temper-store-postgres, cargo clippy -p temper-store-postgres --all-targets -- -D warnings, full cargo test -p temper-store-postgres -- --nocapture, projection worktree git diff --check, report inline JavaScript syntax check, and report git diff --check all pass.

2026-05-16 15:48 EDT

Implemented PERF-005B locally. The dispatch API now has an explicit await_reactions option; all default/OData/platform call sites preserve existing inline behavior, while put_file_stream_content_native opts into bounded background File reactions after the blob write and File transition commit. The background path emits reaction.dispatch.background and keeps overload behavior conservative by awaiting inline when no semaphore permit is available.

2026-05-18 03:34 EDT

Created startup repair worktree /Users/seshendranalla/Development/temperpaw-worktrees/startup-paw-agent-bundle-20260518 from origin/main at 8bfb5fd2, branch codex/startup-paw-agent-bundle-20260518. Added a red regression test that parses every os-apps/paw-agent/specs/*.ioa.toml file and asserts get_os_app("paw-agent") loads the catalog.

The red failure identified the root cause: session.ioa.toml had two inline owners for trigger emit_ots_trajectory, one under FinalizeResult and one under FinalizeResultNoReply. That made RecordResult's trigger effect ambiguous across owners and invalidated the bundle.

2026-05-18 03:40 EDT

2026-05-18 05:31 EDT

Datadog narrowed PERF-021B from a broad TemperPaw Session/OData idea into a concrete Temper runtime dispatch fix. The routed proof trace 9d0317bdbf78eeb763235a443d9fe34a shows WASM host calls to openpaw-production.up.railway.app/tdata/SessionEntries and child OData/entity spans around 80-130 ms. Because that host is the same service, the request is leaving the process through Railway and re-entering the same OData path.

Decision: treat this as an overlooked local-dispatch boundary rather than a fundamental Temper architecture limit. The existing ADR-0099 local TData path was correct for loopback URLs; PERF-021B extends that idea to explicit same-service public origins without bypassing OData, Cedar, event history, projections, or OTS.

APM endpoint latency refresh Over the latest live two-hour window, long-lived event stream spans were 45-60 s but almost entirely idle_ns. Excluding those streams, file $value writes were about 2.1-2.4 s, one WASM probe was about 1.3 s, policy creation was about 0.4 s, and regular OData/list reads were mostly 6-20 ms. The prior diagnosis still applies: ordinary reads are already fast on the projection plane, while blob/file writes, integration/WASM work, and selected control-plane work need separate optimization lanes. Raw APM averages must exclude long-lived streams or they overstate server work. Add dashboard filters/notes that separate streaming spans from request/response spans; keep slow-file and WASM paths in the next optimization queue after measurement deploy.

AuthZ candidate filter Temper PR #239 and TemperPaw PR #270 are merged, CI/Docker are green, Railway deployment 8ad1c472-1c63-4473-a9ed-10f7458df253 is live, and Datadog shows candidate metrics under version:7726e4dfba5453c337dafb7411f88c239f3d5bd5. Datadog native profiler path Production currently has DD_PROFILING_ENABLED=false, so scripts/temperpaw-entrypoint.sh does not wrap the process in ddprof. Current Datadog docs recommend ddprof for compiled languages including Rust, C, and C++ when runtime, symbols, OS permissions, service/env/version, and Agent/export settings are correct. The in-process profiler is a proven fallback/control for targeted captures, but ddprof remains the likely first-class continuous profiler for Temper's Rust process if Railway permissions and image contents support it. Add a deployment task to install/wrap with ddprof, verify perf_event_paranoid/capabilities and symbols, then compare native continuous profiles with the in-process fallback.

audit / isolation

Review Ledger

How To Isolate This Work

Each material improvement was split into its own main-based worktree and PR. Temper core changes usually have a matching TemperPaw rollout PR that pins the new Temper revision, runs TemperPaw CI/Docker, and deploys the image. This ledger is the review index for another agent: inspect the PR bundle, ADR, merge commit, deployment proof, and Datadog proof row-by-row instead of trying to infer the program from one large branch.

Current rule: every new product or observability improvement remains a separately reviewable PR bundle. If the change lives in Temper core, the bundle normally has two PRs: the Temper implementation PR and the TemperPaw rollout PR that deploys the pinned revision. Report-only/status PRs are kept in the ledger but should not be counted as latency improvements.

00 / direct answer

Does The Previous Diagnosis Still Apply?

Yes, with a production update

The central conclusion still holds: Temper's verified transition table and actor admission path are not the main latency limit in the observed production system. The slow areas are around the mission-preserving core: authorization, projection/index maintenance, event persistence shape, WASM integrations, blob transfer, and observability cardinality.

The important update is that production Datadog traces show a newer projection read plane: entity_catalog, entity_field_index, and dispatch.phase.query_projection. Those symbols are not present in this local checkout, so Datadog is the fresher source for production behavior.

Measured approach

We should treat speed as a correctness program. First repair measurement: profiling, DBM, percentile metrics, trace budgets, and projection drift observability. Then optimize the CPU and write-amplified paths with repeatable load tests and replay parity.

keep verified specs keep Cedar default-deny keep event audit bound projection lag control trace cardinality make profiling continuous

Layer What Datadog showed Interpretation Confidence
Transition table / actor admission Actor spawn and admission p95 were sub-millisecond. Local JIT transition evaluation is microseconds. The mission core is not the current latency limit. High
Read/query plane OData GET p95 was around 17 ms using projection tables. The new projection plane largely fixed regular read latency. High
Projection writes Query projection tails into seconds during fan-out; one trace emitted 104k field-index insert spans. Write amplification and trace fan-out are primary architecture issues. High
Cedar authorization AuthZ p95 around 200 ms; sampled trace was almost entirely busy CPU time. Needs profiling, context dimensions, cache/precompile strategy. High
WASM/external integrations Session integration actions had p50s of seconds and p95s of tens of seconds. External workflow work needs queueing, budgets, and async progress semantics. High
Postgres DBM DBM sees temperpaw-postgres as healthy and fresh post-repair plans include real payloads for entity_catalog, entity_field_index, and events. Instance health and plan-level diagnosis are now usable; schema collection and track_activity_query_size remain follow-up gaps. Medium
Profiling Production on-demand CPU profile under read load produced a valid 26.4 KB pprof payload, uploaded to the Datadog Agent, and exposed fresh profile_type:cpu upload buckets. Targeted CPU profiling is useful now; continuous profiling, native ddprof, wall/allocation/lock coverage, and freshness monitors still need completion. Medium
03 / diagnosis

Architecture Versus Overlooked Costs

Architecture constraints to preserve

  • Specs are generated from conversation and verified before deployment.
  • Cedar remains default-deny and auditable.
  • Events remain durable enough for replay, audit, evolution, and proof.
  • Tenant isolation remains a first-class key in stores, caches, and metrics.
  • The deterministic simulation boundary remains clean.

Likely overlooked costs

  • Per-field projection writes and row-by-row index maintenance.
  • Per-call AuthZ context cloning and Cedar evaluation CPU.
  • Event append shape with SELECT MAX(sequence_nr).
  • Blob upload path copying bytes before blob_adapter.
  • Observability cardinality: huge traces and average-only metrics.
Cause and remedy mapmission-safe speed
flowchart LR
  Slow["Latency pressure"] --> Auth["AuthZ CPU"]
  Slow --> Proj["Projection write amp"]
  Slow --> Integrations["External/WASM workflows"]
  Slow --> Blob["Blob data plane"]
  Slow --> DB["DB transaction shape"]
  Slow --> Obs["Observability overhead/gaps"]
  Auth --> AuthFix["profile -> compile/cache -> avoid clones"]
  Proj --> ProjFix["diff -> batch -> selective index -> drift monitor"]
  Integrations --> IntFix["durable executor -> idempotent callbacks"]
  Blob --> BlobFix["direct upload or true streaming"]
  DB --> DBFix["pool wait + tx duration + CAS append"]
  Obs --> ObsFix["distributions + exemplars + trace budgets"]
  AuthFix --> Mission["Verified + governed + fast"]
  ProjFix --> Mission
  IntFix --> Mission
  BlobFix --> Mission
  DBFix --> Mission
  ObsFix --> Mission
04 / observability

Coverage And Gaps

What is good today

  • APM has meaningful phase spans for dispatch, projection, Cedar, WASM, and SQL.
  • SQL spans include busy/idle clues that distinguish actual query time from waiting.
  • DBM has produced projection/event/trajectory query samples and post-repair explain plans in earlier windows, while APM SQL spans remain dependable during the latest proof.
  • Custom metrics exist for Cedar phase timing, Postgres pool/transaction timing, projection queue/e2e timing, dispatch, WASM, trace sampling, and profiling uploads.
  • Dashboard and monitor coverage exists, including DBM, Cedar, WASM, actor, mailbox, trace-budget, and standard APM rate/error/duration signals.

What is not good enough

  • Targeted CPU profiling worked previously, but the current 35f55a93 deployment has no fresh profile proof, so profiler usefulness must be re-proven before CPU-heavy PERF-017 work.
  • DBM plan collection is partially repaired, but PERF-014 through PERF-016 trace-to-DBM correlation returned sparse or missing instance/sample evidence; schema collection, CPU/vacuum coverage, track_activity_query_size tuning, and the events anti-join candidate remain open.
  • Formal SLO objects are not yet defined, even though p95/p99 metrics and monitors now exist.
  • WASM metrics are percentile-enabled, but temper_wasm_invocation_duration_ms cannot currently be grouped by module_name, hiding the key latency dimension in metric views.
  • Projection correctness instrumentation exists, but scheduled/live drift, replay parity, and version/hash checks need production execution.
  • Service dependency graph did not cleanly show temperpaw -> temperpaw-postgres.

Question Current answer Needed instrumentation
Is Datadog profiling instrumented? Yes for targeted in-process CPU profiling. Production routes, Agent upload, upload counters, and dashboard widgets exist; the latest live run captured a parseable pprof payload and Datadog upload activity with zero upload errors. Railway currently has DD_PROFILING_ENABLED=false and TEMPER_DDPROF_ENABLED=false, so continuous/native profiling is not active. Continuous capture/native ddprof remains a canary path, and that canary also depends on Railway accepting a redeploy. Keep targeted CPU captures as the reliable fallback; still prove allocation/lock profile support or document Rust/Railway limits.
Is profiling useful right now? Useful for targeted CPU captures under real load. Fresh proof on 2026-05-18 captured /_admin/profile/cpu, emitted one profiler-upload series point, and emitted no upload-error series. It is not yet enough for always-on diagnosis because continuous profiling is disabled in production variables and native ddprof remains a redeploy-gated canary path. Use targeted CPU captures for Cedar, JSON, projection diffing, and WASM host overhead. Enable the continuous profiler canary only once Railway deploys are available again; if it fails, record the exact Railway perf-permission limit.
Is DB monitoring useful? Useful for query-count/query-time health and APM SQL correlation. Latest Datadog snapshot returned DBM query-count/time series for service:temperpaw,database_instance:temperpaw-postgres and recent APM SQL spans with trace/span ids. DBM activity rows remain sparse, so they are diagnostic only, not the integration-alive gate. Use DBM query metrics plus APM SQL spans for measured query-shape work; keep the monitor based on postgresql.queries.count. Still fix/decide schema collection, CPU/vacuum/wait coverage, and track_activity_query_size.
Can we prove projections are correct? Partially, and now with a stronger local proof. We can emit source-tagged projection queue/update/backfill/applied-sequence metrics, opt into sampled catalog shadow reads, and run a replay parity verifier that compares active catalog rows with state rebuilt from the event journal. Scheduled/live parity execution, row-count parity, hash/version parity, deployed dashboard proof, and clean drift/error monitors.

Keep the verified core

Do not replace the actor/state-machine model. Optimize persistence, authorization, projections, and external work around it.

Move derived work out

Projection and integration work should be bounded, coalesced, and observable. Synchronous semantics should be explicit.

Instrument correctness

If reads depend on projections, drift detection, replay parity, and read-your-write guarantees belong in dashboards.

  P-->>C: version + row counts

  S->>P: projected sample

OBS-001 / profiler restoration

Make Datadog profiling continuously useful. Targeted production CPU profiling is live-proven under load; continuous/native profiling is packaged but currently blocked or canary-only on Railway.

  • Local: added profiler config, capture, freshness, upload, and continuous-mode metrics.
  • Local: added opt-in continuous CPU capture with Datadog Agent auto-upload requirement.
  • Live: verified /_admin/profile/cpu under authenticated read load; Datadog received fresh CPU upload counters and the pprof file parses locally.
  • Live: latest proof-helper run captured /_admin/profile/cpu?seconds=5&frequency=100 in 5.10s, returned application/vnd.google.protobuf, produced an 83-byte pprof payload, and observed fresh datadog.profiling.rust.profiles_uploaded activity with no upload errors.
  • Live: capability endpoint reports TEMPER_DDPROF_ENABLED=false, ddprof_present=true, perf_event_paranoid=3, and CAP_PERFMON=false; Datadog continuous profiler uploads did not appear in the latest window.
  • Next: run the continuous profiler as a controlled canary only after Railway accepts deployments again; until then, use targeted captures for suspected CPU paths and document the Railway permission limit if native continuous profiling cannot be enabled safely.
  • Next: confirm allocation/lock profile path or document Rust profiler limitations.

Acceptance: either fresh profiles within 10 minutes for the canary path, or an explicit documented platform limit plus a repeatable targeted-profile fallback.

Warm direct five-sample proof completed 5/5 with client p50 1977 ms, min 1971 ms, max 2023 ms. Channel proof created Session ss-019e36fb-7ae1-74c3-89fb-ce77bcdd026e, preserved reply_channel_entity_id=en-019e36fb-72d1-7190-ab64-95807f72b9d9, and dispatched the reply in 2486 ms.
  • Datadog proof: warm direct trace 3b9cd163e2cc67625d7ca0bf8ccb0e64 shows service.version=166538703356d488dbfad0b65e8c09f20839dfc9, no error spans, no correlated error logs, Session.workflow 3.442 s, workspace_provisioner 453 ms, bootstrap_new_workspace 225 ms, context_preparer 312 ms, provider_caller 263 ms, provider_response_applier 377 ms, agent_reply 193 ms, emit_ots_trajectory 229 ms, and RecordResult.integrations 422 ms. PERF-011 baseline b91ff7ad9a427641356cc1a4e2da0f55 was Session.workflow 3.711 s, workspace_provisioner 602 ms, and bootstrap_new_workspace 370 ms.
  • Channel Datadog proof: trace ce3cef48c1b6e1a690eb1b35de215772 shows agent_reply sending directly to /tdata/Channels('en-019e36fb-72d1-7190-ab64-95807f72b9d9')/Paw.Channel.SendReply in 460 ms, with no agent_reply GET /tdata/Channels lookup. The remaining channel reply drag is the send action itself, not channel discovery.
  • Measured conclusion: the targeted local bootstrap work improved, and the route correctness gap closed, but user-visible latency did not materially move. PERF-012 therefore teaches that the next win must remove repeated stage envelopes and same-process OData/HTTP work across the Session turn, not just batch the first two SessionEntry writes.

PERF-013 / direct terminal bypass

Remove the direct no-route terminal delivery envelope selected by PERF-012 evidence. Direct API/mock Sessions currently invoke agent_reply even though that module immediately proves there is no channel route and skips; the warm PERF-012 trace measured that no-op WASM envelope at 193 ms.

  • Status: complete for ADR-015. Commit 5fd4baa0 was pushed, PR #285 passed PR CI run 25998641692, merged as df66cbc1, passed main CI 25998916318, passed Docker 25998916310 with image ghcr.io/nerdsane/temperpaw:sha-df66cbc, deployed to Railway as 8597d7eb-7e50-47d8-8814-c404891a83dd, and is version-correct at sha-df66cbc.
  • Decision: add RecordResultNoReply from ApplyingProviderResponse to Completed. It mirrors RecordResult result/accounting/cleanup params and still triggers emit_ots_trajectory, but it does not trigger deliver_reply.
  • Routing guard: provider_response_applier chooses the bypass only when reply_channel_id, reply_thread_id, reply_route_source, and parent_session_id are empty, and agent_id is empty or equal to the Session id. Every ambiguous or channel-bound shape keeps RecordResult.
  • Correctness boundary: event audit, SessionEntry append, token accounting, pending tool/approval cleanup, terminal CompletedHasResult, Cedar callback authorization, and OTS trajectory emission remain intact. Channel delivery and DeliveryFailed behavior are untouched.
  • Local proof: provider_response_applier tests 11/11, Session architecture tests 16/16, Session lifecycle/config tests 6/6, full cargo test --locked -p temperpaw --quiet (190 tests), staged-turn cutover script, rustfmt, package check, package clippy, full paw-agent WASM build, and diff whitespace check are green.
  • Direct live proof: perf-013-direct-noreply-20260517185016 completed in 2776 ms with event path Created -> Configure -> ProvisionWorkspace -> WorkspaceReady -> ContextReadyAuthSkipped -> ProviderResponseReady -> RecordResultNoReply -> MarkTrajectoryEmitted. Datadog trace 2c38b98f5ecec3ffc0fc94d3a1334dd9 shows Session.workflow 4.082 s, RecordResultNoReply.integrations 339.9 ms, emit_ots_trajectory, and no direct agent_reply leg.
  • Routed live proof: Channel en-019e3748-442d-7783-bb0f-61e723f3112b, ChannelSession en-019e3748-4960-7aa3-85eb-d4e65245e781, and Session ss-019e3748-48ef-7a41-b3bb-24320e6ee73d preserved route fields, used RecordResult, invoked agent_reply, called Paw.Channel.SendReply, and delivered webhook payload PERF013_ROUTED_OK 20260517185223.
  • Measured outcome: the narrow correctness-safe bypass works, but the full direct Session path is still measured in seconds. The PERF-014 Datadog refresh showed the first post-deploy proof was partly cold, so the next slice first moves hot-module WASM compile/blob fetch to startup. If warm latency remains high after that proof, the next larger change should be same-process host APIs, a provider-only composite executor, further staging collapse, or a DB/projection cleanup selected by trace and DBM evidence.

Move cold compile and persisted WASM blob fetch for the provider-only Session path off the first live turn after deploy or restart.

PERF-015 / local WASM TData host path

Remove the same-process TCP loopback for safe WASM GET/POST calls to Temper's own /tdata endpoint, while continuing to use the existing OData handlers and correctness checks.

  • Status: live proof complete with mixed latency outcome. Fresh Temper worktree /Users/seshendranalla/Development/temper-worktrees/latency-local-wasm-http-host-20260517, branch codex/latency-local-wasm-http-host-20260517, commit f598ed18, PR #251 merged as 6d09be4d; TemperPaw rollout PR #287 merged as d230d3c2; Railway deployment 957fd772 is live and version-correct on sha-d230d3c2.
  • Decision: ADR-0099 chooses an in-process host wrapper for loopback /tdata calls. The wrapper must sit inside the existing AuthorizedWasmHost chain so WASM HTTP authorization still gates the call before OData executes.

  • Correctness rule: do not create a SessionEntry side channel, skip read-after-write verification, or collapse the Session state machine in this slice. This is transport-shape work, not semantics work.

PERF-024 / SessionEntry materialization read-back compression

Reduce the post-provider first-turn materialization tail without weakening SessionEntry correctness or deterministic reconstruction.

  • Status: production complete, accepted as read-shape/correctness cleanup, not as a latency win. Worktree /Users/seshendranalla/Development/temperpaw-worktrees/session-entry-materialization-tail-20260518, branch codex/session-entry-materialization-tail-20260518, based on origin/main at 70c83302. ADR-023 is written; PR #302 merged as 5342702 and deployed as sha-5342702.
  • Baseline: fixed-version trace 281a8c195707f8149dd6b52a29cde73a shows Session.workflow=2833.2 ms including explicit 2001.2 ms drain, Session.ProviderResponseReady.integrations=324.252 ms, and wasm:provider_response_applier=324.145 ms. The fixed-version aggregate window 2026-05-18T22:56Z..23:10Z confirms the same one-sample Session hot-path envelope and shows general odata.entity_set_read p95 around 16.4 ms with one unrelated Taxonomies outlier.
  • Diagnosis: first-turn SessionEntry POSTs already use ctx.http_call_batch, and the pinned Temper SDK default batch host runs requests concurrently. The likely overlooked cost is the verification fanout: the batched materialization path verifies each expected EntryId with a separate filtered projection read, paying repeated guest/host, OData, projection, and response parsing overhead for the same Session.
  • Implementation: kept concurrent POST batching; replaced the batched initial-materialization read-back fanout with one session-scoped SessionEntries read; parsed the returned OData value array; required every expected EntryId before returning success; preserved bounded retries for projection lag. Single-entry append keeps its narrower per-entry verification.
  • Local verification: focused/full wasm-helpers tests (30/30), provider_response_applier tests (13/13), session_turn_architecture (21/21), Datadog contract (32/32), cargo check --locked -p temperpaw, cargo clippy --locked -p temperpaw --all-targets -- -D warnings, full cargo test --locked -p temperpaw, root rustfmt, diff whitespace, and release WASM builds for provider_response_applier plus workspace_provisioner.
  • Rollout verification: PR CI run 26066826031 passed; PR merged as 5342702; main CI 26067379543 and Docker 26067379531 passed; Railway deployment 911b3396 succeeded; /healthz, /readyz, and /paw/version are green on sha-53427029.
  • Live correctness proof: Session ss-019e3d9c-f7ad-7ed2-a96f-17fb0db9b888 returned PERF024_READBACK_OK_20260519002235, completed in 1191.7 ms, and read back exactly header 0, user 1, assistant 2. Extra samples ss-019e3da2-521e-76d1-8316-61d8b1f2bb76, ss-019e3da2-5a95-72c1-bd13-8bda3fbd7684, and ss-019e3da2-6300-70b1-8eaf-0cb81fd9e9dc completed in 1264.1 ms, 1277.4 ms, and 1232.7 ms with the same 0/1/2 invariant.
  • Before/after Datadog result: before trace 281a8c... had Session.workflow=2833.2 ms, temper.workflow.drain_grace=2001.2 ms, Session.ProviderResponseReady.integrations=324.252 ms, wasm:provider_response_applier=324.145 ms, and SessionEntries shape 3 POST + 4 GET total with 3 provider-materialization read-back GETs. After traces 506355..., 8edfbe..., and 85d1eb... aggregate to Session.workflow avg 3000.2 ms, temper.workflow.drain_grace avg 2001.0 ms, Session.ProviderResponseReady.integrations avg 386.5 ms, wasm:provider_response_applier avg 386.3 ms, and SessionEntries shape 3 POST + 2 GET total per trace. Conclusion: correctness and fanout improved; latency did not.

PERF-025 / context-preparer prompt metadata batching

Reduce first-turn context-preparation wall time by batching independent prompt metadata reads while preserving prompt content, ordering, and fallback behavior.

  • Status: complete, measured, and not counted as a speed win. Worktree /Users/seshendranalla/Development/temperpaw-worktrees/session-context-prep-fast-path-20260518, branch codex/session-context-prep-fast-path-20260518, based on origin/main at 53427029. ADR-024 is written at os-apps/paw-agent/adrs/024-context-preparer-prompt-read-batching.md. Commit 58a8fd06 merged through PR #303 as dae25573, deployed to Railway as sha-dae2557.
  • Baseline: after-PERF-024 traces 506355a6e9104036022a440cece4fb9e, 8edfbe8ff36c14e1eeb2a3626d6cb8ea, and 85d1ebc823044e3ae7e840cb125a4bd9 show wasm:context_preparer avg 237.5 ms and Session.WorkspaceReady.integrations avg 237.7 ms. The inspected trace logs load_messages=12 ms, assemble_system_prompt=74 ms, and a prompt cache miss.
  • Diagnosis: prompt assembly issued independent reads for project harness, system/project/agent skill indexes, and active memory serially. Each read is usually small, but the serial shape stacks guest/host, OData, projection, and JSON parsing overhead inside the first-turn user path.
  • Implementation: context_preparer now builds an ordered prompt metadata batch, sends it through ctx.http_call_batch(&requests), parses responses into project-harness, skill, and memory blocks, then appends those blocks in the original semantic prompt order. If the batch host call fails or returns the wrong response count, the code logs context_preparer: prompt metadata batch unavailable and uses the previous serial helpers.
  • Correctness guard: soul, agent instructions, explicit override, project harness, skills, plan/active plan, memory, and SDK reference keep their existing prompt order. Skill precedence remains agent > project > system. skills_prompt_mode=index still advertises names/paths/workspace IDs only; full/body modes may still read bodies after index discovery.
  • Local verification: context_preparer unit tests (13/13), focused prompt-batch tests (2/2), full session_turn_architecture (21/21), native_skill_installation (5/5), Datadog SDK pin contract (1/1), staged_turn_cutover.sh, cargo fmt --all -- --check, cargo clippy --locked -p temperpaw --all-targets -- -D warnings, git diff --check, and context_preparer wasm32-unknown-unknown release build.
  • Rollout proof: PR CI passed; post-merge main CI run 26070518069 passed; Docker run 26070517901 passed and published sha-dae2557; Railway deployment b95fffee is SUCCESS; /healthz, /readyz, and authenticated /paw/version are green.
  • Live proof: Session ss-019e3df5-00ba-7f51-bb3d-d48bda61da1c returned PERF025_CONTEXT_BATCH_OK_20260519015847, carried the skill-index prompt block, and read back SessionEntries 0/1/2. Four additional production mock Sessions completed with the same 0/1/2 invariant.
  • Before/after Datadog result: before sha-5342702 traces averaged wasm:context_preparer=237.5 ms, Session.WorkspaceReady.integrations=237.7 ms, and inspected assemble_system_prompt=74 ms. After sha-dae2557 traces averaged wasm:context_preparer=345.9 ms, Session.WorkspaceReady.integrations=346.1 ms, Session.ProviderResponseReady.integrations=620.4 ms, and prompt assembly logs 39.7 ms. Query for context_preparer: prompt metadata batch unavailable returned 0 logs. Conclusion: the inner assembly sub-step improved, but the product-level span did not.
  • Next learning: small metadata batching is not a guaranteed user-visible win in the current host path. Before another micro-batch, add finer host-batch/OData timing or attack a larger remaining tail: terminal materialization, OTS/reply work, or the explicit workflow root drain.

PERF-026 / headerless first-turn SessionEntries

Reduce the post-provider first-turn materialization tail by removing the version-only SessionEntry header row from the virtual hot path, while preserving user/assistant audit rows, deterministic reconstruction, and read-back verification.

  • Status: complete and accepted as a measured latency win. Worktree /Users/seshendranalla/Development/temperpaw-worktrees/session-entry-append-fast-path-20260518, branch codex/session-entry-append-fast-path-20260518, based on origin/main at dae25573. ADR-025 is written at os-apps/paw-agent/adrs/025-headerless-first-turn-session-entries.md. Commit 05c04383 became PR #304, which passed PR CI, merged as 8c726f17, passed main CI and Docker, deployed to Railway as 9f94ea68, and live-proved on sha-8c726f17.
  • Baseline: after-PERF-025 on service.version=dae25573e50a752dd105ea3c0986c3bdc5f5b770, window 2026-05-19T00:00Z..03:00Z, Session.ProviderResponseReady.integrations averaged 620.4 ms with p95 672.0 ms, wasm:provider_response_applier averaged 620.2 ms with p95 671.9 ms, and append_session_tree logs sampled 284, 306, 387, 358, 384, 398, and 364 ms (avg 354.4 ms).
  • Trace detail: e7afb8... shows wasm:provider_response_applier=529.0 ms, append_session_tree=306 ms, three internal materialization POST spans around 218, 156, and 149 ms, and a session-scoped read-back GET around 21.6 ms. This points at POST count and OData/materialization host work as the next measured target.
  • Decision: the first-turn header row is version-only metadata, not conversational content. In the virtual hot path, the user entry can be the root row with empty ParentEntryId in OData, parentId=null in JSONL, and Sequence=1; the assistant entry remains its child with Sequence=2. Legacy header/user/assistant trees remain supported by the reader.
  • Implementation: materialize_initial_session_entries_with_assistant now creates and verifies only user/assistant for fresh virtual first turns. The existing next_session_entry_id convention still maps generated first user IDs to logical parent sequence 1, so the first assistant becomes sequence 2. Single-entry append remains unchanged and still uses per-entry read-back.
  • Correctness guard: session_entries_materialized=true remains gated on read-back visibility; the verifier still performs exact EntryId checks from one session-scoped read; context reconstruction accepts both headered and headerless root-user trees; event audit, Session result state, OTS trajectory, and tenant/Cedar behavior are unchanged.
  • Local verification: focused headerless helper/tree tests, full wasm-helpers (31/31), full session-tree-lib (11/11), full provider_response_applier (13/13), focused and full session_turn_architecture (21/21), full cargo test --locked -p temperpaw --quiet (198 tests), release wasm32-unknown-unknown build for provider_response_applier, cargo fmt --all -- --check, cargo clippy --locked -p temperpaw --all-targets -- -D warnings, and git diff --check.
  • Live proof: Session ss-perf026-20260519033028 completed in 1139.0 ms, returned PERF026_HEADERLESS_PROOF_20260519033028, and read back exactly two entries: root user u-ss-perf026-20260519033028-0 with empty parent/Sequence=1, and assistant a-2 with user parent/Sequence=2. Eight batch Sessions ss-perf026-batch-20260519033313-01 through -08 completed and asserted the same shape.
  • After measurement: on service.version=8c726f172e709f862b01b5b9c6ed5c1e9ccdd10d, window 2026-05-19T03:25Z..03:50Z, Session.ProviderResponseReady.integrations averaged 290.1 ms with p95 297.4 ms, wasm:provider_response_applier averaged 290.1 ms with p95 297.4 ms, and append_session_tree averaged 136.9 ms with min/max 130/151 ms. Outcome: about 53% faster provider-response envelope and about 61% faster append tail.

PERF-027 / data-only entity create fast path

Remove framework overhead from immutable/data-only entity creation without weakening Temper's mission: event journal remains authoritative, projections are acknowledged before 201, tenant/write prechecks stay in OData, and action-bearing entities keep the actor path.

  • Status: deployed and correctness-proven, but not accepted as a latency win. Temper worktree /Users/seshendranalla/Development/temper-worktrees/data-only-create-fast-path-20260519, branch codex/data-only-create-fast-path-20260519, based on origin/main at 19d34f0a. ADR-0105 is written at docs/adrs/0105-data-only-entity-create-fast-path.md. Commit f87b9170 opened PR Temper #258, which passed CI and merged as de081598.
  • Rollout: fresh main-based TemperPaw worktree /Users/seshendranalla/Development/temperpaw-worktrees/bump-temper-data-only-create-20260519, branch codex/bump-temper-data-only-create-20260519, based on origin/main at 8c726f17. Commit 46e9abd2 opened PR TemperPaw #305; PR CI passed and the rollout merged as 8dcbe10c. Post-merge main CI 26078759076 and Docker 26078759079 passed; Railway deployment e10ea3e8-78e8-4a02-830c-a9908c8b557e is live on sha-8dcbe10c. No new ADR is required for this dependency-only rollout; the architecture decision lives in ADR-0105.
  • Baseline: after-PERF-026 on service.version=8c726f172e709f862b01b5b9c6ed5c1e9ccdd10d, window 2026-05-19T03:25Z..03:50Z, Session.ProviderResponseReady.integrations averaged 290.1 ms with p95 297.4 ms, wasm:provider_response_applier averaged 290.1 ms with p95 297.4 ms, and append_session_tree averaged 136.9 ms.
  • Trace detail: 11156646544924625715 shows wasm:provider_response_applier=290.6 ms, wasm.invoke.run=145.0 ms, two overlapped POST /tdata/SessionEntries spans around 109.7 ms and 77.9 ms, and matching entity.get_or_create_tenant_entity work. This means the next tail is actor/bootstrap/read-back overhead for two no-transition rows, not provider content or Discord delivery.
  • Decision: eligibility is generic and spec-derived. The fast path only applies when the live TransitionTable.rules list is empty. It does not hardcode SessionEntry, Recorded, TemperPaw, or any product entity name.
  • Implementation: handle_odata_post now calls try_create_data_only_tenant_entity after tenant extraction, verification gate, and write prechecks, and after PG-backed actor creation is excluded. Eligible creates append the same sequence-1 Created event, write the query projection, update entity_index, emit the same observe/SSE state-change event, and return the normal annotated entity response without spawning an actor or asking GetState.
  • Correctness guard: duplicate or concurrent creates that hit an optimistic concurrency violation fall back to the existing actor-backed path, preserving current idempotent behavior. Projection failure is still surfaced as a request failure, so callers never accept a durable event that is invisible in the read model without being told to retry/repair.
  • Local and push verification: focused cargo test -p temper-server data_only -- --nocapture passed 2/2. The positive test creates a no-rule LogEntry through OData, verifies no actor was hydrated, reads the row from projection, then hydrates an actor and proves replay returns Status=Recorded, Sequence=1, and the original field. The guard test proves an action-bearing Order returns None from the fast path and stays on the actor path. Full cargo test -p temper-server, focused OS-app catalog-race tests, DST/code-quality review markers, diff whitespace, full pre-push pipeline, and GitHub CI passed.

PERF-028 / acknowledged SessionEntry create verification

Target the residual exposed by PERF-027: first-turn materialization already batches the user and assistant SessionEntry creates, but the hot path still pays an immediate filtered read-back before returning from provider_response_applier. PERF-028 keeps the write governed and correctness-checked while removing that default extra read.

  • Status: implemented, merged, deployed, live-proofed, and measured. Worktree /Users/seshendranalla/Development/temperpaw-worktrees/session-entry-ack-verify-20260519, branch codex/session-entry-ack-verify-20260519, commit 2ee67ae6, based on origin/main at 8dcbe10c. ADR-017 is written at os-apps/paw-agent/adrs/017-session-entry-acknowledged-create.md. PR TemperPaw #306 merged as 7dec6778ff1a11efba907903c41de690e258076b and deployed as sha-7dec677.
  • Before baseline: PERF-027 after trace 958e12... on service.version=8dcbe10c8b4b257e2e968c7662ab1e7a16e74cb2 shows Session.ProviderResponseReady.integrations=402.7 ms, wasm:provider_response_applier=402.6 ms, and two data-only create spans whose busy time is under 1 ms each.
  • Decision: parse every successful create response and require the returned entity state to contain the exact requested SessionId and EntryId. If the response is malformed or mismatched, the WASM fails closed. The strict legacy read-back remains opt-in through session_entry_create_verify_readback=true in entity fields or WASM config.
  • Correctness boundary: the write still goes through public OData handling, tenant/runtime headers, write prechecks, event append, data-only projection acknowledgement, and normal OData response shaping. The skipped part is only the immediate filtered read-back inside the same WASM helper; live proof after deployment must still perform an independent SessionEntry query.
  • Local and CI checks: failing tests were added first for response-ack parsing and strict read-back mode; then wasm-helpers passed 34/34, provider_response_applier passed 13/13, release WASM build passed, session_turn_architecture passed 21/21, session_lifecycle_and_config passed 6/6, full cargo test -p temperpaw passed, cargo fmt --all -- --check passed, cargo clippy --locked -p temperpaw --all-targets -- -D warnings passed, and git diff --check passed. PR CI, post-merge main CI, and Docker also passed.
  • Deployment proof: Railway deployment 54807fa8-2ee1-4cdd-8793-c1ab34430f3f is live; /readyz is green; authenticated /paw/version returned {"version":"sha-7dec677","sha":"7dec6778ff1a11efba907903c41de690e258076b"}.
  • Live correctness proof: 18 production Sessions completed across batches perf028-after-20260519074106 and perf028-warm-20260519074242. Independent SessionEntries read-back returned exactly two rows for each Session with exact SessionId matches. Datadog logs found 18 ack-verification events and no proof-window error logs/spans.
  • After measurement: retained warm after spans on service.version=7dec6778ff1a11efba907903c41de690e258076b showed Session.ProviderResponseReady.integrations avg 469.1 ms, p50 469.3 ms, p95 549.6 ms, and wasm:provider_response_applier avg 468.5 ms, p50 467.8 ms, p95 549.0 ms. Representative trace 1ebed9... showed Session.ProviderResponseReady.integrations=485.9 ms, wasm:provider_response_applier=485.6 ms, and two data-only create spans of 53.8 ms and 39.2 ms.
  • Acceptance result: correctness accepted, latency not accepted. PERF-028 proved the safer no-read-back contract, but before 402.7 ms became after warm avg about 469.1 ms, so the next slice must tackle a larger write/projection shape rather than another small verification read.

INC-003 / SessionEntry sequence-order repair

Repair the ordering invariant exposed by PERF-023 so every materialized first turn reconstructs deterministically as header, user, assistant.

  • Status: production-accepted correctness fix, not counted as a speed win. Worktree /Users/seshendranalla/Development/temperpaw-worktrees/session-entry-sequence-order-20260518, branch codex/session-entry-sequence-order-20260518, based on origin/main at 2f7f718b. PR #300 passed PR CI run 26063628906, merged as 70c83302, passed main CI 26064229539, passed Docker 26064229569, and deployed to Railway as fb32e595-c9f7-45d5-a471-f2a6ec46c074.
  • Diagnosis: generated first user IDs use u-<session_id>-0. The helper treated the trailing 0 as a SessionEntry sequence instead of a user-turn index, so the first assistant became a-1 and both user and assistant could store Sequence=1. Sorting by (Sequence, EntryId) can then put assistant before user.
  • Implementation: parent_session_entry_sequence maps generated u-ss-...-0 IDs to logical parent sequence 1, causing the first assistant to become a-2 with Sequence=2. Legacy compact IDs such as u-1, a-17, and t-3 still advance by numeric suffix.
  • Verification: focused next_session_entry_id tests, full wasm-helpers (28/28), provider_response_applier (13/13), session_turn_architecture (21/21), Datadog observability contract (32/32), locked check, locked clippy, full cargo test --locked -p temperpaw, rustfmt, diff whitespace, release WASM builds for provider_response_applier and workspace_provisioner, PR CI, main CI, Docker, Railway deploy, live proof, and Datadog proof.
  • Before/after result: before Session ss-019e3d3e-3603-7790-a6fb-69b039089f50 on sha-2f7f718b completed in 2345.8 ms and read back header 0, assistant a-1/1, user u-...-0/1. After Session ss-019e3d53-1e13-7cd1-aac2-2c79c3c7df1e on sha-70c83302 completed in 1188.5 ms and asserted header 0, user 1, assistant a-2/2.
  • Datadog proof: before trace 1001161827511810271 has root 3439.0 ms including 2001.0 ms drain. After trace 281a8c195707f8149dd6b52a29cde73a has root 2833.2 ms including 2001.1 ms drain and carries service.version=70c83302a213ed11ae834d40fc60fa16678688c6. The apparent elapsed improvement is not claimed as a latency win because the slice is correcting deterministic read-model shape, not optimizing a measured hot span.

INC-002 / projection parity and tenant propagation

Repair two correctness-contract issues before the next latency slice lands: inconsistent unfiltered projection reads and missing tenant headers on internal local TData calls.

  • Status: production-accepted. Temper worktree /Users/seshendranalla/Development/temper-worktrees/projection-read-model-parity-20260518, branch codex/projection-read-model-parity-20260518, based on origin/main at 92c2a728. PR #256 passed CI run 26055526339 and merged as 19d34f0a. TemperPaw rollout worktree /Users/seshendranalla/Development/temperpaw-worktrees/bump-temper-projection-parity-20260518, branch codex/bump-temper-projection-parity-20260518, was based on origin/main at 59489a93 and pins runtime/server/WASM SDK surfaces to 19d34f0a. TemperPaw PR #299 passed PR CI run 26057382473 and merged as 3b8dcc2315dc67fa3a5aa49ef31247aa7f3282b5. Main CI 26058149170 and Docker 26058149128 passed. Railway deployment 82a73a02-73d5-4155-8e55-8be2ed7fd9a5 succeeded on sha-3b8dcc2 with authenticated /paw/version returning sha-3b8dcc23.
  • Projection diagnosis: unfiltered collection reads could enumerate one durable source while filtered reads used projection indexes, allowing impossible answers such as All DesignLanguages = 6 but Published DesignLanguages = 173. This is a read-model parity bug, not a frontend moral failure.
  • Tenant-header diagnosis: the OData multi-tenant handler has long required X-Tenant-Id; the newer local TData host path plausibly exposed missing headers for internal cron/agent calls. The fix injects the actor/invocation tenant only when the guest request omits the header and preserves explicit headers.
  • Implementation: Postgres and Turso list_entity_ids_by_type now read from a union of entity_catalog, entity_field_index, and events, excluding any ID with a Deleted tombstone. OData collection reads record odata.entity_set_read fields for entity set/type, filter pushdown, ID source, catalog materialization, candidate count, materialized count, and returned count.
  • Correctness: unfiltered All now sees the same superset as filtered reads; filtered reads still use pushdown when available; catalog materialization is preferred when a query plane exists so migrated/catalog-only rows do not disappear after ID enumeration.
  • Verification so far: cargo test -p temper-store-turso list_entity_ids_by_type passed 3/3; cargo test -p temper-store-postgres list_entity_ids_by_type_unions_catalog_field_index_and_events passed; cargo test -p temper-server local_tdata passed 10/10; focused clippy passed; report/code diff checks passed; full repository pre-push passed rustfmt, workspace clippy, readability ratchet, workspace tests, and doctests. TemperPaw rollout checks now pass: cargo check --locked -p temperpaw, cargo test --locked -p temperpaw --test datadog_observability_contract (32/32), cargo test --locked -p temperpaw --test session_lifecycle_and_config (6/6), cargo clippy --locked -p temperpaw --all-targets -- -D warnings, cargo fmt --all -- --check, and git diff --check.
  • Live projection proof: production sha-3b8dcc23 returned All DesignLanguages = 240; canonical Status filters returned Draft 41, UnderReview 4, Published 170, and Archived 25; the filtered union had 240 unique IDs and exactly matched the unfiltered ID set. This corrects the observed before shape where All = 6 and Published = 173. Residual follow-up: the proof also showed lowercase status filters are not equivalent for DesignLanguages, so any client that relies on lowercase aliases should be audited separately.
  • Live cron/control-plane proof: proof CronJob cj-019e3cdc-3fe1-75c1-89cb-983cebb94a4d was created, configured, activated, observed with next_run_at=2026-05-19T20:52:08Z, and paused back to Paused on production without tenant-header failure.
  • Datadog acceptance: APM query shows odata.entity_set_read spans carrying tenant, entity_set, filter_pushdown, id_source, candidate_count, materialized_count, and returned_count. Unfiltered DesignLanguages span 230a9dad413c2441af07b447e7cc095a records id_source=read_source_union and returned_count=240; filtered Published span 754d13ebe1a77bcf154b8bc1c013f524 records id_source=filter_pushdown and returned_count=170; CronJob list span a0267d3b4cef0b7d9375db0f9627c746 records returned_count=2. Log analysis for Missing required X-Tenant-Id header since 2026-05-18T20:44Z returned 0 rows.
  • Acceptance result: complete for correctness/stability. This is not counted as a speed improvement; it becomes a guardrail for future speed work. Future projection/cache/materialization changes still require before/after latency data plus drift/read-back correctness proof.

Keep slow integrations from defining the whole platform latency.

  • Move long-running WASM/external calls to a durable executor.
  • Use idempotency keys and verified callback transitions.
  • Emit progress state and queue wait metrics.

PERF-005 / blob data plane

Stop routing large bytes through the hottest control path.

  • Local: ADR-0088 defines a native built-in File $value write fast path.
  • Local: built-in File uploads now store content through native content-addressed blob storage, then dispatch the verified StreamUpdated action.
  • Local: remote content-addressed writes avoid the existing native HEAD-then-PUT shape and use a direct PUT.
  • Local: cargo check -p temper-server and cargo test -p temper-server --test file_value_fast_path passed; OData File $value succeeds without any blob_adapter registered.
  • Live: Temper PR #238 and TemperPaw PR #269 merged, Railway deployed fd83c31b, and production read-back proof passed.
  • Measured: Datadog upload trace moved from 3,159.5 ms with wasm.invoke to 353.3 ms with native state.put_file_stream_content.native and no matching WASM/blob-adapter span.
  • PERF-005B local: ADR-0092 and branch codex/latency-file-value-residual-20260516 target the remaining measured tail where File upload still waits on post-commit reaction fanout.
  • PERF-005B local: dispatch now has explicit await_reactions; all existing generic/OData/platform callers preserve inline reaction semantics, while native File $value writes commit bytes and StreamUpdated synchronously, then run the FileVersion/RecordVersion trigger chain in a bounded background lane.
  • PERF-005B local checks: background trigger test, File $value fast-path test suite, focused adapter/reaction/trigger/WASM suite, cargo check -p temper-server -p temper-platform, clippy for both crates, and the full pre-push gate all pass.

Target: large upload latency scales with storage path, not actor/control-plane CPU.

OBS-005 / trace budgets

Keep traces useful without making them the bottleneck.

  • ADR-0083 defines sampler policy and rollback.
  • Sampler decision/config/rate metrics are implemented.
  • Reaction fanout summary span fields are implemented.
  • Datadog widgets and monitors are ready to deploy.

Target: no routine 100k-span traces while slow/error trace roots and fanout summaries remain available.

VERIFY-001 / replay load lab

Make performance regressions reproducible.

  • scripts/verify-latency-observability-package.sh full passed locally.
  • Run python3 scripts/temper_agent_e2e_proof.py during staging/live proof.
  • Generate synthetic tenants with large entities and many fields.
  • Compare projected reads to event/actor state during load.

Target: every speed claim has a repeatable benchmark and correctness check.

DECIDE / explicit architecture calls

These should become ADR-backed decisions.

  • What read-your-write guarantee does projection promise?
  • Which fields are query-indexed by default?
  • Which integrations must remain inline?
  • What data sizes use direct blob upload?

Target: performance choices are contracts, not accidental behavior.

08 / sources

Sources And Anchors

Repository anchors

  • README.md: verified, policy-driven runtime and high-level architecture.
  • crates/temper-server/src/state/entity_ops.rs: authorize_with_context.
  • crates/temper-server/src/state/dispatch/mod.rs and dispatch/wasm.rs: WASM dispatch.
  • crates/temper-server/src/odata/write.rs: stream PUT $value and blob_adapter.
  • crates/temper-store-postgres/src/store.rs: event append transaction shape.
  • crates/temper-observe/src/wide_event.rs: WideEvent span/metric emission.
  • crates/temper-authz/src/metrics.rs and crates/temper-authz/src/engine/mod.rs: Cedar evaluation and ADR-0084 phase metrics.

Live observability sources

  • Datadog APM spans for temperpaw: OData, dispatch phases, Cedar, projection, WASM, and SQL.
  • Datadog DBM for temperpaw-postgres: query samples, health signals, lock/wait evidence, plan gaps.
  • Datadog metrics: Cedar, AuthZ phase, projection, dispatch, WASM, and profiler upload/error metrics.
  • Datadog monitors and dashboard: TemperPaw - Platform Overview.
  • Vapor Terminal OVA Console design language.

Caveat: production traces include projection symbols absent from this local checkout, so Datadog is treated as the fresher source for production performance.
